超越宫颈阴道局限:人乳头瘤病毒(HPV)感染与口腔、肛门和阴茎部位黏膜微生物群之间的潜在双向相互作用——简要综述

Beyond cervicovaginal limitations: potential bidirectional interactions between HPV infection and mucosal microbiota from oral, anorectal and penile sites — a mini-review
作者： Liu W, Yu Q, Xue X
摘要中文翻译这篇小型综述对当前测序和流行病学证据进行了叙述性综合,涉及男性和女性宫颈阴道外生态位中 HPV 感染与特定位点粘膜微生物群之间潜在的双向相互作用。该综述抵消了长期存在的性别相关和解剖学研究偏见,阐明了多位点 宿主-病毒-微生物机制,并为开发基于微生物群的生物标志物和微生态干预措施以预防非宫颈 HPV 相关恶性肿瘤提供理论支持。6 结论这篇小综述对宫颈阴道组织以外的口腔、肛门直肠和阴茎生态位中 HPV 与粘膜微生物群之间观察到的关联和潜在的双向相互作用进行了叙述性综合。这篇叙述性小型评论整合了两性宫颈阴道外粘膜生态位的现有证据,以减轻历史上以宫颈为中心的研究偏差(表 1)。Tuominen 等人 (2018a) 来自 MOUTH 研究的 OralRisk 登记成人参与者*N=495 横截面唾液;口腔冲洗16S rRNA V4 扩增子测序;PICRUSt2;玉米芯差异丰度分析观察到β多样性的显着变化。大多数关注 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 与微生物群相互作用的现有研究仅限于子宫颈阴道道,而口腔、肛门直肠和阴茎部位的粘膜微生态串扰仍未得到充分总结。口腔 HPV 以性别、种族和人群依赖性方式重塑微生物组成;牙周失调、高危型HPV 和白色念珠菌共同形成三方致癌轴,可能导致口腔鳞状细胞癌,而牙周炎症独立升高口腔 HPV 易感性。在与男性发生性关系的男性中...
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
这篇小型综述对当前测序和流行病学证据进行了叙述性综合,涉及男性和女性宫颈阴道外生态位中 HPV 感染与特定位点粘膜微生物群之间潜在的双向相互作用。
证据基础与方法
阐明宫颈阴道外微生物群与 HPV 感染之间的相关性将进一步阐明粘膜 HPV 易感性和相关的 宿主-微生物-病毒相互作用。健康普通人群口腔HPV总体患病率范围为0.67%至11.89%,男性感染率高于女性(Maltseva et al.,2025)。
共识与结论
观察数据表明,口腔微生物群的成员可能通过改变 HPV 的致病性或细菌群落行为来与 HPV 相互作用(Ghannoum 等,2010;Muzio 等,2021)。大规模 NHANES 横断面数据 (n=5496) 进一步证实,口腔微生物 α 多样性减少和独特的群落特征仅与男性中的高危口腔 HPV 显着相关,这种基于性别的差异与男性对 HPV 相关头颈恶性肿瘤的易感性较高一致(Park 等人,2022 年;Gaeta 等人,2023 年;Feng 等人,2024)。据报道,口腔 HPV 不会诱导独特的口腔微生物分类群或生态型,而是通过协调的毒力网络定殖预先存在的口腔生态系统(Chambuso 等人,2026)。
争议与缺口
这些发现受到样本量小和仅招募怀孕受试者的限制,这限制了这些结论对更广泛人群的普遍适用性。另一项横断面研究分析了 5000 多个口腔冲洗样本,报告显示 HPV 阳性个体中韦荣菌科的相对丰度较高,双粒菌减少。
科研与实践启示
这项工作表明,由 HIV 驱动的先前存在的口腔生态失调掩盖了 hrHPV 的微生物特征,而 hrHPV 通过扩大致病类群进一步扰乱粘膜微生态。据报道,口腔 HPV 阳性孕妇的口腔微生物丰富度较高,其中硒单胞菌属、巨球菌属和 TM7 相关类群的相对丰度较高(Tuominen 等人,2018a)。另外,来自同一组的婴儿口腔微生物群数据显示,口腔 HPV 阳性改变了属和科水平的细菌谱,但没有改变整体 α 多样性(Tuominen 等人,2018b)。

Beyond cervicovaginal limitations: potential bidirectional interactions between HPV infection and mucosal microbiota from oral, anorectal and penile sites — a mini-review

Authors: Liu W, Yu Q, Xue X
Original abstract (English)
This mini-review provides a narrative synthesis of current sequencing and epidemiological evidence regarding potential bidirectional interactions between HPV infection and site-specific mucosal microbiota across extra-cervicovaginal niches in both males and females. This review offsets long-standing sex-related and anatomical research bias, clarifies multi-site host-virus-microbe mechanisms, and provides theoretical support for developing microbiota-based biomarkers and microecological interventions to prevent non-cervical HPV-associated malignancies. 6ConclusionThis mini-review provides a narrative synthesis of observed associations and potential bidirectional interactions between HPV and mucosal microbiota in oral, anorectal and penile niches beyond cervicovaginal tissues. This narrative mini-review integrates available evidence for extra-cervicovaginal mucosal niches in both sexes to mitigate the historical cervix-centered research bias (Table 1). Tuominen et al (2018a) OralRisk-enrolled adult participants from MOUTH study*N=495Cross-sectionalSaliva; oral-rinse16S rRNA V4 amplicon sequencing; PICRUSt2; Corncob differential abundance analysisSignificant shifts in β-diversity were observed. Most existing studies focusing on human papillomavirus (HPV)-microbiota interactions are confined to the cervicovaginal tract, while mucosal microecological crosstalk at oral, anorectal and penile sites remains insufficiently summarized. Oral HPV reshapes microbial composition in a sex-, race- and population-dependent manner; periodontal dysbiosis, high-risk HPV and Candida albicans jointly form a tripartite oncogenic axis that may contribute to oral squamous cell carcinoma, and periodontal inflammation independently elevates oral HPV susceptibility. Among men who have sex with men...
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This mini-review provides a narrative synthesis of current sequencing and epidemiological evidence regarding potential bidirectional interactions between HPV infection and site-specific mucosal microbiota across extra-cervicovaginal niches in both males and females.
Evidence base and method
Elucidating the correlation between extra-cervicovaginal microbiomes and HPV infection will further clarify mucosal HPV susceptibility and relevant host-microbe-viral interactions. The overall prevalence of oral HPV among healthy general populations ranged from 0.67% to 11.89%, and infection rates were higher in males than females (Maltseva et al., 2025).
Consensus and conclusions
Observational data suggest that members of the oral microbiota may interact with HPV by modifying its pathogenicity or bacterial community behavior (Ghannoum et al., 2010; Muzio et al., 2021). Large-scale NHANES cross-sectional data (n=5496) further confirmed that reduced oral microbial α-diversity and unique community profiles were only significantly associated with high-risk oral HPV among males, a sex-based discrepancy consistent with men’s higher susceptibility to HPV-related head and neck malignancies (Park et al., 2022; Gaeta et al., 2023; Feng et al., 2024). It is reported that oral HPV does not induce distinct oral microbial taxa or ecotypes, but colonizes pre-existing oral ecosystems with coordinated virulence networks (Chambuso et al., 2026).
Controversies and gaps
Such findings are constrained by the small sample size and exclusive recruitment of pregnant subjects, which limits the generalizability of these conclusions to broader human populations. Another cross-sectional study analyzing more than 5000 oral-rinse specimens reported higher relative abundance of Veillonellaceae and reduced Gemella among HPV-positive individuals.
Research and practice implications
The work indicates pre-existing oral dysbiosis driven by HIV masks microbial signatures of hrHPV, and hrHPV further disturbs mucosal microecology by expanding pathogenic taxa. Higher oral microbial richness among oral-HPV-positive pregnant women was reported, with elevated relative abundance of Selenomonas, Megasphaera and TM7-related taxa (Tuominen et al., 2018a). Separately, infant oral microbiota data from the same group showed oral HPV positivity altered bacterial profiles at genus and family levels without changing overall α-diversity (Tuominen et al., 2018b).

HPV 驱动的 宿主 染色质结构 宫颈癌:分子机制和治疗耐药性

Host chromatin architecture in HPV-driven cervical cancer: molecular mechanisms and therapeutic resistance
作者： Katsuyoshi Kumagai, Yasuhiro Suzuki, Kei Kitahara, Ayumi Chiba, Hiroaki Yajima
摘要中文翻译高危 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 持续感染是 宫颈癌 的主要病因驱动因素。然而,HPV 状态和 E6/E7 介导的 p53/RB 轴破坏并不能完全解释为什么 HPV 相关病变在持续性、恶性进展、治疗反应和复发方面存在差异。本综述探讨了 宿主 染色质结构和相关转录程序如何有助于 HPV 介导的检查点破坏后恶性细胞状态的稳定。我们讨论了将 HPV 持久性和整合与表观遗传许可性、三维 基因组 组织、增强子-启动子重新布线、粘连蛋白动力学和 WAPL 相关染色质调控联系起来的证据。特别关注这些机制如何影响以 MYC/E2F 为中心的增殖输出,并与背景依赖性信号传导和肿瘤微环境适应配合,以促进 治疗 耐受和抵抗恶性状态。我们还考虑如何使用染色质信息生物标志物、公共数据集、扰动引导优先级、经过验证的 宫颈癌 细胞系、类器官和患者衍生模型来评估治疗相关的脆弱性。通过将病毒肿瘤发生与 宿主 基因组 调节和适应性抵抗相结合,本综述提出了一个概念性和可实验测试的框架,用于对 HPV 驱动的 宫颈癌 进行分层并优先考虑候选干预节点。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
高危 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 持续感染是 宫颈癌 的主要病因驱动因素。然而,HPV 状态和 E6/E7 介导的 p53/RB 轴破坏并不能完全解释为什么 HPV 相关病变在持续性、恶性进展、治疗反应和复发方面存在差异。
证据基础与方法
本综述探讨了 宿主 染色质结构和相关转录程序如何有助于 HPV 介导的检查点破坏后恶性细胞状态的稳定。我们讨论了将 HPV 持久性和整合与表观遗传许可性、三维 基因组 组织、增强子-启动子重新布线、粘连蛋白动力学和 WAPL 相关染色质调控联系起来的证据。
共识与结论
通过将病毒肿瘤发生与 宿主 基因组 调节和适应性抵抗相结合,本综述提出了一个概念性和可实验测试的框架,用于对 HPV 驱动的 宫颈癌 进行分层并优先考虑候选干预节点。
争议与缺口
我们还考虑如何使用染色质信息生物标志物、公共数据集、扰动引导优先级、经过验证的 宫颈癌 细胞系、类器官和患者衍生模型来评估治疗相关的脆弱性。
科研与实践启示
特别关注这些机制如何影响以 MYC/E2F 为中心的增殖输出,并与背景依赖性信号传导和肿瘤微环境适应配合,以促进 治疗 耐受和抵抗恶性状态。

Host chromatin architecture in HPV-driven cervical cancer: molecular mechanisms and therapeutic resistance

Authors: Katsuyoshi Kumagai, Yasuhiro Suzuki, Kei Kitahara, Ayumi Chiba, Hiroaki Yajima
Original abstract (English)
Persistent infection with high-risk human papillomavirus (HPV) is the principal etiologic driver of cervical cancer. However, HPV status and E6/E7-mediated disruption of the p53/RB axis do not fully explain why HPV-associated lesions differ in persistence, malignant progression, therapeutic response, and relapse. This review examines how host chromatin architecture and related transcriptional programs may contribute to the stabilization of malignant cell states following HPV-mediated checkpoint disruption. We discuss evidence linking HPV persistence and integration to epigenetic permissiveness, three-dimensional genome organization, enhancer-promoter rewiring, cohesin dynamics, and WAPL-associated chromatin regulation. Particular attention is given to how these mechanisms may influence MYC/E2F-centered proliferative output and cooperate with context-dependent signaling and tumor microenvironmental adaptation to contribute to treatment tolerance and resistant malignant states. We also consider how chromatin-informed biomarkers, public datasets, perturbation-guided prioritization, authenticated cervical cancer cell lines, organoids, and patient-derived models can be used to evaluate therapeutically relevant vulnerabilities. By integrating viral oncogenesis with host genome regulation and adaptive resistance, this review proposes a conceptual and experimentally testable framework for stratifying HPV-driven cervical cancer and prioritizing candidate intervention nodes.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Persistent infection with high-risk human papillomavirus (HPV) is the principal etiologic driver of cervical cancer. However, HPV status and E6/E7-mediated disruption of the p53/RB axis do not fully explain why HPV-associated lesions differ in persistence, malignant progression, therapeutic response, and relapse.
Evidence base and method
This review examines how host chromatin architecture and related transcriptional programs may contribute to the stabilization of malignant cell states following HPV-mediated checkpoint disruption. We discuss evidence linking HPV persistence and integration to epigenetic permissiveness, three-dimensional genome organization, enhancer-promoter rewiring, cohesin dynamics, and WAPL-associated chromatin regulation.
Consensus and conclusions
By integrating viral oncogenesis with host genome regulation and adaptive resistance, this review proposes a conceptual and experimentally testable framework for stratifying HPV-driven cervical cancer and prioritizing candidate intervention nodes.

Research and practice implications

27-45 岁成年人中 HPV 疫苗 覆盖率:对口咽癌预防的影响

HPV vaccine coverage among adults aged 27–45: Implications for oropharyngeal cancer prevention
作者： Rong Jiang, May Z. Gao, Vincent M. D’Anniballe, Matthew Lu, Tammara L. Watts, Gregory D. Zimet, Darien J. Weatherspoon, Erika L. Thompson, Nosayaba Osazuwa-Peters, Vincent M. D'Anniballe
摘要中文翻译
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
发病率 (HPV) 感染会导致 HPV 相关口咽癌 (OPC) 的 发病率 发病率上升。
证据基础与方法
我们分析了 2019 年和 2022 年 27-45 岁成年人的 NHIS 数据(2019 年 n = 8,745;2022 年 n = 7,303)。我们估计了年龄调整后的调查加权覆盖率,并使用多变量逻辑回归来估计按社会人口特征调整的 疫苗接种 的赔率;2022 年的二次分析评估了 27 岁至 45 岁之间的起始年龄。
共识与结论
Ever HPV vaccine uptake increased from 15.1% in 2019 to 20.7% in 2022 (difference 5.6 percentage points; 95% CI, 4.03-7.07). The primary outcome was ever HPV vaccination (≥1 dose), regardless of age at first vaccination.
争议与缺口
Only 1.43% (95% CI, 1.07-1.80) initiated vaccination between ages 27 and 45 in 2022. The increase in lifetime HPV vaccine coverage likely reflects childhood and adolescent vaccination rather than initiation at ages 27-45, which remained uncommon, and persistent inequities highlight missed opportunities for equitable OPC prevention.
科研与实践启示
2018 年,HPV 疫苗接种 扩大到 27-45 岁的成年人,COVID-19 导致预防性就诊和常规免疫接种大幅下降。

HPV vaccine coverage among adults aged 27–45: Implications for oropharyngeal cancer prevention

Authors: Rong Jiang, May Z. Gao, Vincent M. D’Anniballe, Matthew Lu, Tammara L. Watts, Gregory D. Zimet, Darien J. Weatherspoon, Erika L. Thompson, Nosayaba Osazuwa-Peters, Vincent M. D'Anniballe
Original abstract (English)
Human papillomavirus (HPV) infection contributes to rising incidence of HPV-related oropharyngeal cancer (OPC). expanded HPV vaccination to adults aged 27-45 y in 2018, COVID-19 caused preventive visits and routine immunizations to plunge. We analyzed 2019 and 2022 NHIS data among adults aged 27-45 (2019 n = 8,745; 2022 n = 7,303). The primary outcome was ever HPV vaccination (≥1 dose), regardless of age at first vaccination. We estimated age-adjusted, survey-weighted coverage and used multivariable logistic regression to estimate adjusted odds of vaccination by sociodemographic characteristics; secondary 2022 analyses assessed initiation between ages 27 and 45. Ever HPV vaccine uptake increased from 15.1% in 2019 to 20.7% in 2022 (difference 5.6 percentage points; 95% CI, 4.03-7.07). In both years, lower odds were observed with increasing age, men, non-Hispanic Asian adults, individuals with lower educational attainment, and those without a usual place for care. By 2022, lower income-to-poverty ratios and lack of insurance were associated with reduced odds, whereas LGB+ and unmarried adults had higher odds, and adults in the Northeast had higher odds than those in the South. Only 1.43% (95% CI, 1.07-1.80) initiated vaccination between ages 27 and 45 in 2022. Initiation was more common among females, LGB+ and non-Hispanic Asian adults, and unmarried adults, and less common among those with lower education and no usual place for care. The increase in lifetime HPV vaccine coverage likely reflects childhood and adolescent vaccination rather than initiation at ages 27-45, which remained uncommon, and persistent inequities highlight missed opportunities for equitable OPC prevention.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
Human papillomavirus (HPV) infection contributes to rising incidence of HPV-related oropharyngeal cancer (OPC).
Evidence base and method
We analyzed 2019 and 2022 NHIS data among adults aged 27-45 (2019 n = 8,745; 2022 n = 7,303). We estimated age-adjusted, survey-weighted coverage and used multivariable logistic regression to estimate adjusted odds of vaccination by sociodemographic characteristics; secondary 2022 analyses assessed initiation between ages 27 and 45.
Consensus and conclusions
Ever HPV vaccine uptake increased from 15.1% in 2019 to 20.7% in 2022 (difference 5.6 percentage points; 95% CI, 4.03-7.07). The primary outcome was ever HPV vaccination (≥1 dose), regardless of age at first vaccination.
Controversies and gaps
Only 1.43% (95% CI, 1.07-1.80) initiated vaccination between ages 27 and 45 in 2022. The increase in lifetime HPV vaccine coverage likely reflects childhood and adolescent vaccination rather than initiation at ages 27-45, which remained uncommon, and persistent inequities highlight missed opportunities for equitable OPC prevention.
Research and practice implications
expanded HPV vaccination to adults aged 27-45 y in 2018, COVID-19 caused preventive visits and routine immunizations to plunge.

肿瘤生物组与 HPV 和妇科癌症的关系

Oncobiome relation with HPV and Gynecological cancer
作者： Saman Soleimanpour, Atieh Yaghoubi
摘要中文翻译到目前为止,人们已经充分了解人体居住着数以百万计的微小生物体,这些生物体存在于我们身体的不同部位,称为微生物群。在过去的几十年里,这些微小的微生物像一个与人类健康联系在一起的智能网络一样协同工作,这个网络中的任何改变或生态失调都会导致不同的疾病以及癌症的发展。因此,许多临床前和临床研究都集中在阐明微生物群在癌症发展中的确切作用。其中一种癌症是妇科癌症,它被认为是对女性群体健康的主要威胁。对于妇科恶性肿瘤,人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 的存在和持续感染也很重要。HPV 的持续感染会导致阴道甚至肠道内共生微生物群的改变,从而导致癌症进展。因此,我们的目的是全面回顾目前关于肠道微生物群和阴道微生物群在HPV感染中相互作用的知识,有利于妇科癌症的发展。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
该综述重点关注肠道微生物群和阴道微生物群在 HPV 获得过程中的相互作用及其在促进妇科癌症发展中的作用。核心问题是全面了解这些微生物群相互作用如何影响 HPV 相关疾病。
证据基础与方法
该综述是对探索微生物群与 HPV 之间关系的临床前和临床研究的综合分析。它没有明确报告所使用的数据库、搜索日期或资格标准,但它包括一系列研究微生物群在癌症进展中的作用。
共识与结论
该综述得出的结论是,持续的 HPV 感染会导致阴道和肠道微生物群的改变,这可能会导致癌症进展。它强调了在 HPV 相关疾病的背景下了解这些相互作用的重要性。
争议与缺口
有证据表明,HPV 感染引起的微生物群变化可能导致癌症进展,但这些相互作用的确切机制和程度仍不清楚。需要进行更详细的研究来阐明特定微生物群变化在不同 HPV 相关癌症中的作用。
科研与实践启示
该综述表明,需要进一步研究来探索肠道和阴道微生物群与 HPV 感染之间的具体相互作用。这可能会导致更好的预防 HPV 相关癌症的策略,诊断 和 治疗。

Oncobiome relation with HPV and Gynecological cancer

Authors: Saman Soleimanpour, Atieh Yaghoubi
Original abstract (English)
By now, it is well understood that the human body is inhabited by millions of tiny organisms that are alive and found in different parts of our body that called microbiota. Over the past decades, these tiny microorganisms have worked together like a smart network that links to human health, and any alterations or dysbiosis in this network lead to different disorders as well as cancer development. Hence, many preclinical and clinical studies have focused on clarifying the exact role of microflora in cancer development. One of these cancers is gynecological cancer, which is considered a major threat to health in the female population. In the case of gynecological malignancy, the presence and persistent infection with human papillomavirus (HPV) is also important. Persistent infection with HPV leads to alterations in the commensal population of microbiota in the vagina and even the gut, resulting in cancer progression. Therefore, we aimed to comprehensively review the current knowledge on the interaction between gut microbiota and vaginal microbiota in HPV acquisition, favoring gynecological cancer development.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
The review focuses on the interaction between gut microbiota and vaginal microbiota in HPV acquisition and its role in favoring gynecological cancer development. The central question is to comprehensively understand how these microbiota interactions influence HPV-related diseases.
Evidence base and method
The review is a comprehensive analysis of preclinical and clinical studies that explore the relationship between microbiota and HPV. It does not explicitly report on the databases used, search dates, or eligibility criteria, but it includes a range of studies examining the microbiota's role in cancer progression.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that persistent HPV infection leads to alterations in the vaginal and gut microbiota, which may contribute to cancer progression. It highlights the importance of understanding these interactions in the context of HPV-associated diseases.
Controversies and gaps
The evidence suggests that alterations in microbiota due to HPV infection may lead to cancer progression, but the exact mechanisms and the extent of these interactions remain unclear. There is a need for more detailed studies to clarify the role of specific microbiota changes in different HPV-related cancers.
Research and practice implications
The review implies that further research is needed to explore the specific interactions between gut and vaginal microbiota and HPV infection. This could lead to better strategies for prevention, diagnosis, and treatment of HPV-associated cancers.

致癌病毒的神经并发症:机制、临床表现和新见解。

Neurological Complications of Oncogenic Viruses: Mechanisms, Clinical Manifestations, and Emerging Insights.
作者： Lei Liu
摘要中文翻译致癌病毒因其在恶性转化中的关键作用而被广泛认可。然而,它们引起神经系统疾病的能力相对较少受到关注。越来越多的证据表明,多种致癌病毒,包括人类 T 细胞白血病病毒 1 型 (HTLV-1)、人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV)、乙型肝炎病毒 (HBV)、丙型肝炎病毒 (HCV) 和卡波西肉瘤相关疱疹病毒 (KSHV),与涉及中枢和周围神经系统的广泛神经系统并发症有关。这些表现是通过多种机制产生的,包括直接病毒神经侵袭、持续感染、免疫介导的组织损伤、慢性神经炎症和神经血管完整性破坏。此外,病毒诱导的分子改变,如氧化应激、线粒体功能障碍、表观遗传重塑和异常的细胞因子信号传导,会导致神经元功能障碍和疾病进展。在这篇综述中,我们通过整合分子、实验和临床研究的证据,批判性地研究了目前对与主要致癌病毒相关的神经并发症的理解。我们首先讨论病毒感染引起神经损伤的一般机制,强调嗜神经病毒和非嗜神经病毒之间共享的途径。然后,我们总结了与每种致癌病毒相关的神经学表现和病毒特异性致病机制,强调神经发病机制中常见和独特的分子途径。最后,我们解决当前的诊断挑战、新兴生物标志物和治疗策略,同时确定限制因果解释和临床转化的关键知识差距。全面了解致癌病毒、免疫系统和神经系统之间复杂的相互作用将有助于早期 诊断,改善患者管理,并支持开发针对病毒相关神经系统疾病的靶向治疗方法。
查看综述五要素
范围与问题
本综述重点关注与 人乳头瘤病毒 (HPV) 相关的神经系统并发症,特别研究这些病毒引起神经损伤的机制、其疾病特异性表现以及对 诊断 和 治疗 的影响。
证据基础与方法
该综述整合了分子、实验和临床研究的证据,以严格检查 HPV 的神经系统并发症。它讨论了病毒引起的神经损伤的一般机制,然后总结了病毒特异性的致病机制,强调了常见和独特的分子途径。
共识与结论
该综述的结论是,HPV 可通过多种机制诱发神经系统并发症,包括直接神经侵袭、持续感染、免疫介导的组织损伤、慢性神经炎症和神经血管完整性破坏。这些机制与其他嗜神经病毒和非嗜神经病毒共有。
争议与缺口
该综述确定了限制因果解释和临床转化的关键知识差距,特别是在理解精确的分子途径和特定 HPV 类型在神经系统疾病进展中的作用方面。
科研与实践启示
该综述强调需要进一步研究 HPV、免疫系统和神经系统之间复杂的相互作用,以改进诊断方法、开发有针对性的治疗方法并加强患者管理策略。

Neurological Complications of Oncogenic Viruses: Mechanisms, Clinical Manifestations, and Emerging Insights.

Authors: Lei Liu
Original abstract (English)
Oncogenic viruses are widely recognized for their pivotal role in malignant transformation; however, their capacity to induce neurological disorders has received comparatively less attention. Increasing evidence indicates that several oncogenic viruses, including human T-cell leukemia virus type 1 (HTLV-1), human papillomavirus (HPV), hepatitis B virus (HBV), hepatitis C virus (HCV), and Kaposi's sarcoma-associated herpesvirus (KSHV), are associated with a broad spectrum of neurological complications involving both the central and peripheral nervous systems. These manifestations arise through diverse mechanisms, including direct viral neuroinvasion, persistent infection, immune-mediated tissue injury, chronic neuroinflammation, and disruption of neurovascular integrity. Moreover, virus-induced molecular alterations, such as oxidative stress, mitochondrial dysfunction, epigenetic remodelling, and aberrant cytokine signalling, contribute t neuronal dysfunction and disease progression. In this review, we critically examine the current understanding of the neurological complications associated with major oncogenic viruses by integrating evidence from molecular, experimental, and clinical studies. We first discuss the general mechanisms by which viral infections induce neurological injury, highlighting pathways shared among neurotropic and non-neurotropic viruses. We then summarise the neurological manifestations and virus-specific pathogenic mechanisms associated with each oncogenic virus, emphasising both common and distinct molecular pathways underlying neuropathogenesis. Finally, we address current diagnostic challenges, emerging biomarkers, and therapeutic strategies while identifying critical knowledge gaps that limit causal interpretation and clinical translation. A comprehensive understanding of the complex interactions between oncogenic viruses, the immune system, and the nervous system will facilitate earlier diagnosis, improve patient management, and support the development of targeted therapeutic approaches for virus-associated neurological disorders.
View review five-element analysis
Scope and question
This review focuses on the neurological complications associated with human papillomaviruses (HPV), specifically examining the mechanisms by which these viruses induce neurological injury, their disease-specific manifestations, and the implications for diagnosis and treatment.
Evidence base and method
The review integrates evidence from molecular, experimental, and clinical studies to critically examine the neurological complications of HPV. It discusses general mechanisms of viral-induced neurological injury and then summarizes virus-specific pathogenic mechanisms, emphasizing both common and distinct molecular pathways.
Consensus and conclusions
The review concludes that HPV can induce neurological complications through various mechanisms, including direct neuroinvasion, persistent infection, immune-mediated tissue injury, chronic neuroinflammation, and disruption of neurovascular integrity. These mechanisms are shared with other neurotropic and non-neurotropic viruses.
Controversies and gaps
The review identifies critical knowledge gaps that limit causal interpretation and clinical translation, particularly in understanding the precise molecular pathways and the role of specific HPV types in neurological disease progression.
Research and practice implications
The review highlights the need for further research into the complex interactions between HPV, the immune system, and the nervous system to improve diagnostic methods, develop targeted therapeutic approaches, and enhance patient management strategies.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

中国沿海地区男性 HPV 感染率高,疫苗 差距持续存在 - Bioengineer.org

Male HPV Infections Run High in Coastal China, With Vaccine Gaps Persisting - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报A study conducted in coastal China, published on October 6, 2026, highlights the high prevalence of male HPV infections, with over one-third of 731 male outpatients infected with at least one HPV genotype. The research, reported in BMC Infectious Diseases, found that the most common genotypes among men were HPV6, HPV52, HPV16, HPV51, and HPV42, which include both low-risk and high-risk types. High-risk HPV types such as HPV16 are linked to cancers, and the study revealed that 33.5 percent of HPV-positive men carried genotypes not covered by the 9-valent vaccine, indicating a significant coverage gap. The findings emphasize the importance of expanding HPV vaccination to boys and men to address these gaps and reduce transmission risks, as well as the potential role of dermatology clinics in HPV screening. The study also noted that the prevalence of HPV infections in men was highest among those over 60 years old, suggesting that age-related factors may influence infection rates.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2023年5月至2026年2月进行,新闻于2026年10月6日发布。
地点与对象
该研究以中国沿海莆田地区的男性门诊患者为对象,共有 731 名参与者。
事件
一项回顾性研究发现,中国沿海地区三分之一以上的男性门诊患者至少感染一种HPV基因型,其中最常见的是HPV6、HPV52、HPV16、HPV51和HPV42。该研究发表在《BMC 传染病》杂志上。
规模、影响与风险
The overall prevalence of HPV infection among men was 35.2 percent, with 22.7 percent having single-genotype infections and 12.5 percent having multiple-genotype infections. High-risk HPV types such as HPV52, HPV16, and HPV51 were associated with anogenital and oropharyngeal cancers, and the study highlighted that 33.5 percent of HPV-positive men carried at least one genotype not covered by the 9-valent vaccine.
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究采用聚合酶链式反应扩增结合流通杂交方法来检测和鉴定21种不同的HPV基因型。研究结果表明,需要扩大 HPV 传播 计划,将男孩和男性纳入其中,以解决覆盖范围差距并降低 传播 风险。

Male HPV Infections Run High in Coastal China, With Vaccine Gaps Persisting - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study conducted in coastal China, published on October 6, 2026, highlights the high prevalence of male HPV infections, with over one-third of 731 male outpatients infected with at least one HPV genotype. The research, reported in BMC Infectious Diseases, found that the most common genotypes among men were HPV6, HPV52, HPV16, HPV51, and HPV42, which include both low-risk and high-risk types. High-risk HPV types such as HPV16 are linked to cancers, and the study revealed that 33.5 percent of HPV-positive men carried genotypes not covered by the 9-valent vaccine, indicating a significant coverage gap. The findings emphasize the importance of expanding HPV vaccination to boys and men to address these gaps and reduce transmission risks, as well as the potential role of dermatology clinics in HPV screening. The study also noted that the prevalence of HPV infections in men was highest among those over 60 years old, suggesting that age-related factors may influence infection rates.

HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Tuesday, October 6, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Male HPV Infections Run High in Coastal China, With Vaccine Gaps Persisting by October 6, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Male HPV Infections Run High in Coastal China, With Vaccine Gaps Persisting Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Human papillomavirus is often discussed as a women’s health issue, dominated by the burden of cervical cancer, but a new retrospective study from southeastern China adds to a growing body of evidence that men carry a substantial and underappreciated share of the viral load. Researchers at the Affiliated Hospital of Putian University and the First Hospital of Putian City analyzed samples from 731 male outpatients treated between May 2023 and February 2026 and found that more than one in three was infected with at least one HPV genotype. The overall prevalence of 35.2 percent, reported in BMC Infectious Diseases, included single-genotype infections in 22.7 percent of patients and multiple-genotype infections in 12.5 percent, a pattern that underscores how frequently men harbor several viral types simultaneously. The technical approach behind these numbers is worth noting. Rather than relying on a single broad screening assay, the team used polymerase chain reaction amplification combined with flow-through hybridization, a method that allows simultaneous detection and identification of 21 distinct HPV genotypes. This matters because HPV is not one pathogen but a family of more than 200 related viruses, only some of which are classified as high-risk for cancer. Distinguishing which genotypes circulate in a given population is essential for predicting how well existing vaccines will perform and for designing regional screening programs that target the types actually present. The five most frequently detected genotypes among the men were HPV6, HPV52, HPV16, HPV51, and HPV42. This list mixes low-risk and high-risk types: HPV6 and HPV42 are classified as low-risk, typically associated with genital warts rather than malignancy, while HPV52, HPV16, and HPV51 belong to the high-risk category linked to anogenital and oropharyngeal cancers. HPV16 is the single most carcinogenic genotype worldwide and a dominant driver of HPV-positive cancers in both sexes. The fact that it ranks among the top types in these male outpatients reinforces why vaccination and, eventually, male screening remain central to cancer prevention efforts. Discover more Infections Find Vaccines vaccines vaccine Vaccine One of the study’s most striking findings is the overlap between the genotypes circulating in men and those previously reported in local women in the Putian region. The predominant types in the male cohort, including low-risk HPV6 and HPV42 and high-risk HPV52, HPV51, and HPV16, substantially mirror the genotype distribution described for females in the same area. Such concordance is consistent with sexual transmission within partner networks and suggests that vaccinating or screening only one sex leaves a persistent viral reservoir in the other. The authors argue that this overlap supports including men in HPV prevention strategies and may inform future evaluation of partner-oriented prevention approaches, in which couples are considered jointly rather than in isolation. Age emerged as a significant axis of variation. Prevalence was highest among patients older than 60 years, a pattern that contrasts with the classic bimodal age distribution often described in women, where infection peaks in young adulthood and again around midlife. In men, clearance of infection can be less efficient with age, and cumulative exposure over a lifetime may raise the probability of detectable infection in older age groups. The researchers also conducted sensitivity analyses using broader age categories, combining the 51-to-60 and over-60 groups, to confirm the robustness of the association between older age and high-risk HPV infection, indicating that the age signal was not an artifact of how the categories were drawn. Discover more Health health Support Pride Clinical diagnosis also shaped the picture. HPV prevalence varied significantly across the six diagnostic subgroups into which patients were stratified: warts, urinary tract infections, dermatitis, genital diseases, health check-ups, and others. The warts group showed the highest infection rate at 48.7 percent, dominated by HPV6 and HPV11, the two low-risk genotypes most classically responsible for condyloma acuminatum, or genital warts. That result is biologically coherent, since patients presenting with visible warts are, by definition, already expressing an HPV-driven lesion. More unexpected was the dermatitis group, which came in a close second at 46.5 percent and displayed remarkable genotype diversity, with HPV52, HPV16, HPV42, HPV51, and HPV59 all appearing among the detected types. The dermatitis finding carried enough statistical weight to survive multivariable regression analysis. After adjusting for other variables, dermatitis was identified as an independent risk factor for high-risk HPV infection, with the authors reporting an adjusted odds ratio and controlling for confounding using generalized variance inflation factors to check for collinearity among predictors. The biological interpretation is not settled from these data alone, but plausible mechanisms include disruption of the skin barrier providing an easier entry point for virions, local immune dysregulation reducing viral clearance, or inflammatory conditions serving as a marker for behaviors or exposures that also raise HPV risk. Whatever the direction of causality, the association suggests that dermatology clinics could represent an underused point of contact for HPV testing in men. Discover more Book a Checkup Check Symptoms infection Study Biology infections The vaccine analysis is where the study’s public health implications become most concrete. China’s regional vaccination programs have historically focused on girls and women, and the 9-valent HPV vaccine, which protects against genotypes 6, 11, 16, 18, 31, 33, 45, 52, and 58, is the broadest formulation in widespread use. Yet even against this nine-type panel, the researchers calculated that 33.5 percent of HPV-positive men in the cohort carried at least one genotype not covered by the vaccine, and the coverage gap was nearly identical, at 33.9 percent, among men carrying high-risk types. In other words, roughly one third of infections, including those with cancer-associated potential, would not be prevented by the current nonavalent vaccine, a gap driven by types such as HPV51 and HPV59 that fall outside the formulation. These coverage gaps do not diminish the case for vaccination, but they do complicate it. On one hand, the predominance of HPV6, HPV16, and HPV52 in the male cohort means the 9-valent vaccine would still address a majority of circulating infections, and HPV16 in particular is responsible for a disproportionate share of HPV-related cancers. On the other hand, the persistence of a substantial non-vaccine reservoir means that vaccination alone cannot eliminate transmission in this population, and the finding strengthens arguments for extending vaccination to boys and men, both to protect them directly against cancers and warts and to build herd protection that benefits unvaccinated partners. The study has limitations inherent to its design. It was retrospective and drew exclusively on male outpatients at a single hospital in Putian, a prefecture-level city in Fujian Province, so the 35.2 percent prevalence cannot be generalized to the general male population, which typically shows lower rates than clinic-based cohorts. Patients visiting dermatology or urology clinics are more likely to have conditions related to or coinciding with HPV infection than men sampled at random. The ethics committee of the First Hospital of Putian City approved the study and granted an exemption from individual informed consent, and the authors declare no competing interests. Even with those caveats, the work fills a genuine gap: no previous research on male HPV prevalence in Putian had been reported, and region-specific genotype data of this kind are scarce across much of China. The broader takeaway is that HPV epidemiology in men deserves the same granular, genotype-level attention that has long been applied to women. As vaccination programs expand and as oropharyngeal and anal cancers linked to HPV rise in many countries, understanding which types circulate in male populations, at which ages, and in association with which clinical conditions becomes essential for modeling transmission, forecasting vaccine impact, and deciding whether and when to introduce routine male screening. The Putian data, with their high prevalence, distinctive age gradient, dermatitis signal, and measurable vaccine gap, offer a template for the kind of local surveillance that global HPV control efforts will increasingly require. 沉默 HPV16 癌基因 E5 和 E6 成为对抗 宫颈癌 的一条有前途的途径 - Bioengineer.org

Silencing HPV16 Oncogenes E5 and E6 Emerges as a Promising Route Against Cervical Cancer - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报大不里士医科大学的研究人员及其合作者在《病毒学杂志》上发表了一篇新的叙述性评论,探讨了沉默 HPV16 癌基因 E5 和 E6 作为 宫颈癌 有前景的治疗方法的潜力。该研究强调了这些癌蛋白在促进细胞增殖、免疫逃避、细胞凋亡抵抗和基因组不稳定性方面的关键作用,这些是 HPV 介导的恶性转化的关键驱动因素。该综述评估了支持 RNA 干扰和基于 CRISPR/Cas 的策略来靶向这些癌基因的临床前证据,旨在恢复肿瘤抑制功能并增强化疗和放疗等常规疗法的有效性。尽管这些方法显示出潜力,但高效递送和最小化脱靶效应等挑战仍然存在。研究结果表明,针对病毒癌基因可以为传统癌症 治疗 提供更特异、毒性更小的替代方案,与更广泛的精准肿瘤学运动保持一致。然而,证据并未报告具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露数量,而是侧重于生物学机制和治疗意义。

大不里士医科大学的研究人员及其合作者在《病毒学杂志》上发表的一篇叙述性评论讨论了沉默 HPV16 癌基因 E5 和 E6 作为 宫颈癌 治疗策略的潜力。这是基于临床前证据的经证实的科学审查。
规模、影响与风险
该综述强调了 HPV16 癌基因 E5 和 E6 在 宫颈癌 进展中的重要作用,包括促进细胞增殖、免疫逃避、细胞凋亡抵抗和基因组不稳定。然而,证据中没有报告具体的病例数、死亡数或暴露人数。

该综述介绍了临床前研究结果,并讨论了 RNA 干扰和基于 CRISPR/Cas 的策略沉默 HPV16 癌基因的潜力。它承认将这些发现转化为临床应用的挑战,包括递送效率和脱靶效应。来源是著名的科学出版物,证据基于研究,而不是 暴发 或公共卫生事件。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Silencing HPV16 Oncogenes E5 and E6 Emerges as a Promising Route Against Cervical Cancer - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A new narrative review published in Virology Journal by researchers at Tabriz University of Medical Sciences and collaborators explores the potential of silencing HPV16 oncogenes E5 and E6 as a promising therapeutic approach for cervical cancer. The study highlights the critical roles of these oncoproteins in promoting cell proliferation, immune evasion, resistance to apoptosis, and genomic instability, which are key drivers of HPV-mediated malignant transformation. The review evaluates preclinical evidence supporting RNA interference and CRISPR/Cas-based strategies to target these oncogenes, aiming to restore tumor suppressor functions and enhance the effectiveness of conventional therapies like chemotherapy and radiotherapy. While these methods show potential, challenges such as efficient delivery and minimizing off-target effects remain. The findings suggest that targeting viral oncogenes could offer a more specific and less toxic alternative to traditional cancer treatments, aligning with the broader movement toward precision oncology. However, the evidence does not report specific case counts, death counts, or exposure numbers, focusing instead on the biological mechanisms and therapeutic implications.

HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Friday, October 9, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Biology Silencing HPV16 Oncogenes E5 and E6 Emerges as a Promising Route Against Cervical Cancer by October 9, 2026 in Biology Reading Time: 5 mins read 0 Silencing HPV16 Oncogenes E5 and E6 Emerges as a Promising Route Against Cervical Cancer Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Cervical cancer remains one of the most significant global health challenges of our time, and its strongest molecular driver is well established: persistent infection with high-risk human papillomavirus, above all genotype 16. A new narrative review published in Virology Journal by researchers at Tabriz University of Medical Sciences and collaborators takes a close look at two of the virus’s most consequential weapons, the oncoproteins E5 and E6, and surveys how modern gene-silencing technologies could be harnessed to neutralize them. The work, led by Zahra Zenderuh Ravanlo and corresponding author Hossein Bannazadeh Baghi, synthesizes the biological roles of these viral proteins in carcinogenesis and evaluates the preclinical evidence behind RNA interference and CRISPR/Cas-based strategies aimed at shutting them down. The logic behind targeting E5 and E6 rests on their central position in HPV-mediated malignant transformation. According to the review, these oncoproteins collectively promote cell proliferation, immune evasion, resistance to apoptosis, and genomic instability, the four pillars on which a virus-infected cell can progress toward cancer. E5 contributes to tumor progression by modulating growth factor signaling and promoting cell survival, effectively creating a cellular environment in which abnormal proliferation is favored. E6, by contrast, plays the more notorious role: it drives malignant transformation primarily by tagging the tumor suppressor p53 for degradation, thereby removing one of the cell’s most important safeguards against uncontrolled division and DNA damage. The significance of p53 degradation cannot be overstated. In normal cells, p53 acts as a guardian of the genome, halting the cell cycle when DNA damage is detected and triggering programmed cell death when the damage is irreparable. When HPV16 E6 orchestrates the destruction of p53, infected cells lose this critical checkpoint and accumulate mutations with impunity. Because this mechanism is virus-encoded rather than a mutation in the host genome itself, it presents an attractive therapeutic window: a treatment that silences E6 could, in principle, allow p53 function to be restored in tumor cells while leaving normal cells untouched. This selectivity is precisely what makes HPV oncogenes such compelling drug targets compared with conventional cytotoxic approaches. Discover more Learn Engineering Track Biotech Tour Colleges Cancer cancer E5, though less studied than E6, is far from a bystander in the carcinogenic process. The review emphasizes that E5’s modulation of growth factor signaling and its promotion of cell survival help sustain tumor growth and progression. By interfering with normal growth regulatory pathways, E5 helps create conditions in which cells proliferate even in the absence of appropriate external signals. It also contributes to the ability of infected cells to evade immune surveillance, a hallmark of cancer that allows premalignant lesions to persist and progress undetected. A therapeutic strategy that addresses both E5 and E6 simultaneously could therefore attack the disease from multiple angles at once, undermining both the survival signaling and the tumor suppressor evasion that sustain the malignancy. The technological core of the review concerns two families of gene-silencing tools. The first is built on small interfering RNAs, or siRNAs, short double-stranded RNA molecules that exploit a natural cellular pathway to degrade messenger RNA in a sequence-specific manner. When designed to match sequences within the E5 or E6 transcripts, siRNAs can direct the cellular RNA interference machinery to destroy the viral messages before they are translated into protein, effectively silencing the oncogenes at the post-transcriptional level. The second family comprises CRISPR/Cas-based genome-editing systems, which go a step further by introducing targeted disruptions into the viral DNA itself, potentially knocking out the oncogene sequences permanently rather than merely dampening their expression. According to the review, both approaches have demonstrated significant potential in preclinical studies. Suppressing E5 and E6 expression has been shown to restore tumor suppressor functions, induce apoptosis in cancer cells, and enhance the sensitivity of those cells to conventional therapies such as chemotherapy and radiotherapy. This last point carries particular clinical weight. Cervical cancer treatment currently relies on surgery, radiation, and cytotoxic drugs, modalities that are effective but blunt and often accompanied by substantial toxicity. If gene-silencing can sensitize tumor cells to existing treatments, lower doses might achieve the same effect, reducing side effects while improving outcomes. The restoration of p53-mediated apoptosis following E6 silencing offers a mechanistically coherent explanation for these observations: cells that regain the ability to die on cue are inherently more vulnerable to DNA-damaging therapies. The appeal of targeting viral oncogenes rather than host genes also extends to specificity. Because E5 and E6 exist only in HPV-infected cells, therapies directed against their transcripts or coding sequences should, in theory, spare uninfected tissue entirely. This stands in sharp contrast to most anticancer agents, which discriminate between tumor and normal cells only imperfectly. The review frames this viral specificity as a foundational advantage of the gene-silencing paradigm in HPV-driven cancers, one that aligns with the broader movement toward precision oncology in which molecular definitions of disease replace anatomical ones. Discover more Health Biology Study Biology Health research findings Electronic health records Yet the path from laboratory promise to clinical reality is far from straightforward, and the authors devote considerable attention to the challenges that remain. Delivering siRNAs or CRISPR components efficiently and selectively to cervical tumor cells is a formidable obstacle; RNA molecules are rapidly degraded in the bloodstream and do not cross cell membranes unaided, while gene-editing systems require delivery vectors whose safety and tropism must be carefully controlled. Off-target effects, in which silencing or editing tools act on unintended sequences, represent another safety concern that must be rigorously addressed. The review also highlights the inherent limitations of current preclinical models, which may not fully recapitulate the complexity of human cervical carcinogenesis, and notes that therapeutic limitations and unresolved questions still separate these experimental approaches from approved treatments. Despite these hurdles, the therapeutic perspectives outlined in the review are notably optimistic. The authors position targeted molecular approaches against HPV oncogenes as a frontier for both the prevention and treatment of cervical cancer, complementing rather than replacing existing interventions. Prophylactic HPV vaccines have transformed prevention efforts worldwide, but they do not treat established infections or existing lesions, and a large population of women worldwide carries persistent infections that vaccines cannot address. Therapeutic gene silencing fills precisely this gap, offering a potential intervention for individuals whose disease is already established. The combination of vaccination for prevention and oncogene-directed silencing for treatment could, in principle, cover the entire spectrum of HPV-associated disease. The review, which was supported by the Infectious and Tropical Diseases Research Center at Tabriz University of Medical Sciences, ultimately serves as both a status report and a roadmap. It consolidates the mechanistic case that E5 and E6 are pivotal to HPV16-driven carcinogenesis, catalogs the preclinical evidence that siRNA and CRISPR/Cas systems can suppress their expression with meaningful biological consequences, and candidly maps the delivery, safety, and translational challenges that stand between the laboratory and the clinic. As gene-silencing technologies mature and delivery platforms improve, the prospect of a therapy that disarms the very genes the virus uses to cause cancer moves from theoretical appeal toward practical possibility, offering hope for a future in which cervical cancer is not only preventable by vaccine but also treatable at its viral roots. 怀孕前感染 HPV 疫苗接种 与降低早产和其他主要并发症的风险有关

HPV vaccination before pregnancy linked to lower risk of preterm birth and other major complications
新闻简报A new study published in The BMJ investigated the link between HPV vaccination before pregnancy and the risk of pregnancy complications. Researchers at Karolinska Institutet analyzed data from Swedish registers covering 624,713 pregnancies among first-time mothers aged 16 to 35 between 2006 and 2023. They found that women who received the quadrivalent HPV vaccine prior to pregnancy had a lower risk of complications, including preterm birth, premature rupture of membranes, and stillbirth. Specifically, vaccinated women were around five per cent less likely to give birth prematurely and 15 per cent less likely to give birth very prematurely. The study observed stronger associations among women vaccinated before the age of 17. However, as an observational study, it could not definitively conclude that the lower risk was caused by the vaccine. The findings suggest that HPV vaccination may offer benefits beyond cancer prevention, potentially reducing pregnancy-related complications at a population level.
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究进行时间较长,涵盖2006年至2023年的怀孕情况。文章发表日期为2026-10-07。
地点与对象
该研究基于瑞典的登记数据,纳入了 624,713 名年龄在 16 岁至 35 岁之间的初为人母的孕妇。
事件
《英国医学杂志》发表的一项研究发现,接种 HPV 疫苗的女性出现妊娠和分娩并发症的可能性较小,尤其是早产。研究结果表明,怀孕前接种 HPV 疫苗接种 可能会降低早产、胎膜早破、婴儿小于胎龄、死产和新生儿 死亡率 等并发症的风险。
规模、影响与风险
The study found that vaccinated women were around five per cent less likely to give birth prematurely and around 15 per cent less likely to give birth very prematurely. These findings indicate a potential population-level benefit of HPV vaccination in reducing pregnancy complications, though the study was observational and could not establish causation.
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是观察性的,没有确定因果关系,因为研究人员不能排除影响结果的其他因素。然而,它基于大量妊娠并覆盖很长一段时间,为 HPV 疫苗接种 的潜在益处提供了重要见解。

HPV vaccination before pregnancy linked to lower risk of preterm birth and other major complications

News Brief
A new study published in The BMJ investigated the link between HPV vaccination before pregnancy and the risk of pregnancy complications. Researchers at Karolinska Institutet analyzed data from Swedish registers covering 624,713 pregnancies among first-time mothers aged 16 to 35 between 2006 and 2023. They found that women who received the quadrivalent HPV vaccine prior to pregnancy had a lower risk of complications, including preterm birth, premature rupture of membranes, and stillbirth. Specifically, vaccinated women were around five per cent less likely to give birth prematurely and 15 per cent less likely to give birth very prematurely. The study observed stronger associations among women vaccinated before the age of 17. However, as an observational study, it could not definitively conclude that the lower risk was caused by the vaccine. The findings suggest that HPV vaccination may offer benefits beyond cancer prevention, potentially reducing pregnancy-related complications at a population level.
Original source text (English)
The benefits of the HPV vaccine may extend beyond protection against cancer. In a study published in The BMJ, researchers at Karolinska Institutet found that women who had been vaccinated against HPV were less likely to experience several pregnancy and childbirth complications, particularly preterm birth. HPV (human papillomavirus) is the leading cause of cervical cancer. HPV infection and treatment of cervical cell changes have also been associated with an increased risk of certain pregnancy complications. A new study has investigated the link between the HPV vaccine and complications such as preterm birth, premature rupture of membranes, babies being small for their gestational age, stillbirth, and neonatal mortality. The study is based on Swedish registers and covers 624,713 pregnancies among first-time mothers aged 16 to 35 between 2006 and 2023. 92,620 of these women had received the quadrivalent HPV vaccine prior to becoming pregnant, providing protection against four common types of HPV. Stronger link when vaccinated at a younger age Women who had been vaccinated before pregnancy consistently had a lower risk of the studied complications, particularly preterm birth. Compared with unvaccinated women, they were around five per cent less likely to give birth prematurely and around 15 per cent less likely to give birth very prematurely. "We also observed that the associations were stronger among women who had been vaccinated before the age of 17," says Zhongsong Zhang, doctoral student at the Department of Medical Epidemiology and Biostatistics, Karolinska Institutet. "This is consistent with previous findings that the HPV vaccine provides the best protection against HPV-related disease when administered at a younger age." A large register-based study Although the researchers took into account several factors that could have influenced the results, as this was an observational study, it was not possible to conclude that the lower risk was caused by HPV vaccination. Nevertheless, the study covered a large number of women from the Swedish population over a long period of time. "Even a relatively small reduction in the risk of preterm birth can be significant at a population level, given that HPV vaccination is administered to millions of young people worldwide," says Jiayao Lei, assistant professor at the same department at Karolinska Institutet. "Our results suggest that the benefits of HPV vaccination may extend beyond cancer prevention to include the reduction of complications that can arise during pregnancy and childbirth." The study was carried out in collaboration with researchers at the University of Gothenburg, Sahlgrenska University Hospital and the Norwegian Institute of Public Health. Funding for the research came from the Swedish Research Council, the Swedish Research Council for Health, Working Life and Welfare, the Swedish Cancer Society, Karolinska Institutet and ALF grants, among others. Source: Journal reference

Scale, impact and risk
The study found that vaccinated women were around five per cent less likely to give birth prematurely and around 15 per cent less likely to give birth very prematurely. These findings indicate a potential population-level benefit of HPV vaccination in reducing pregnancy complications, though the study was observational and could not establish causation.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study was observational and did not establish causation, as the researchers could not rule out other factors influencing the results. However, it was based on a large number of pregnancies and covered a long time period, providing significant insights into the potential benefits of HPV vaccination.

一名3岁儿童脸上长出尖锐湿疣;医生说这是由于共用毛巾造成的。- 越南.vn

A 3-year-old child developed genital warts on her face; doctors say it was due to sharing a towel. - Vietnam.vn
新闻简报A 3-year-old child in Vietnam developed genital warts on her face, according to a report published on 2026-10-08. Doctors linked the condition to the family's habit of sharing towels, which allowed the virus to spread from the grandfather, who had contracted HPV after visiting a red-light district. The case highlights the risk of non-sexual transmission of HPV through shared personal items. Dr. Gu Fangyu, a urologist in Taiwan (China), noted that while genital warts can be treated, they often recur, emphasizing the need for ongoing monitoring. The incident underscores the importance of hygiene practices in preventing HPV spread, particularly within families. Although not part of a larger outbreak, the case serves as a reminder of the potential for HPV transmission through non-sexual means and the necessity of vaccination and awareness.

事件
一名3岁的孩子脸上长出了尖锐湿疣,医生将此归因于家人共用毛巾的习惯。祖父在逛红灯区后被确认为感染源。
规模、影响与风险
该案涉及一名儿童和一名祖父。顾方宇博士明确提到了复发的风险,并通过共用毛巾的传播强调了对其他家庭成员的潜在风险。

通过追踪感染源,确定了病因。医生强调需要持续监测和治疗。该案例不是更大的 暴发 的一部分,而是突出显示了特定的 传播 路线。

Original source text (English)
A 3-year-old child developed genital warts on her face; doctors say it was due to sharing a towel. The family panicked when genital warts appeared on the boy's face; after a thorough examination, doctors determined that the boy contracted the disease through the family's habit of sharing towels. ZNews • 08/10/2026 This clinical case was shared by Gu Fangyu, a renowned urologist in Taiwan ( China ). The patient, only 3 or 4 years old, had developed pointed warts – commonly known as genital warts – on their face. After tracing the source of infection, the cause was determined to be the habit of sharing towels within the family. The boy's grandfather contracted HPV after visiting a red-light district. Because the family shared towels, the virus spread to his grandson. The grandmother was furious when she learned the cause of the incident. Khám phá thêm Tìm hiểu lịch sử Đọc Mẹo Du Lịch Khám phá ĐNA According to Doctor Gu Fangyu, young patients can still be treated for genital warts, but the more worrying aspect is the risk of recurrence in the future. Therefore, in addition to adhering to treatment, continuous monitoring is also essential at this stage. A 3-4 year old boy developed genital warts on his face, possibly related to the habit of sharing towels within the family. Boys are recommended to receive the HPV vaccine after turning 9 years old (according to Taiwan's HPV vaccination guidelines, the appropriate age range for vaccination is from 9 to 45 years old, and vaccination can be done through designated programs or at their own expense). Social Issues - At age 18, many young people begin serious romantic relationships and become curious about sex. However, many still believe that sexually transmitted diseases are something far-fetched, only affecting those with complicated sex lives. In reality, even a single instance of unprotected sex can have long-lasting health consequences. Unsafe sex led to a nearly 70-year-old male patient contracting genital warts. A male patient, nearly 70 years old, came to the Central Dermatology Hospital for examination due to the appearance of genital warts in the perianal area for about two months, after self-treating at home without success. HPV transmission routes are increasing rapidly in men and younger age groups. There is a clear trend of increasing HPV infection among men, young people, and men who have sex with men (MSM). This has been observed at the National Dermatology Hospital recently. Genital warts – also known as cauliflower-like genital warts – are caused by infection with the HPV (human papillomavirus). In an informative article on her clinic's website, Dr. Gu Fangyu explains that this disease is primarily transmitted sexually, has a long incubation period, and a high recurrence rate. The lesions most commonly appear in the genital or anal region, but genital warts can also develop on other areas of the skin or mucous membranes. The disease can start at any age, but is more common in sexually active individuals. Unsafe sexual practices, multiple sexual partners, and prolonged immunosuppression increase the risk of infection. Even if the body is infected, abnormal signs may not appear immediately. When they first form, genital warts are usually very small and difficult to detect with the naked eye. Furthermore, they often do not cause pain or itching in the early stages, with an incubation period lasting from about 1 to 8 months. Therefore, the disease can exist for a period of time without obvious symptoms. Dr. Gu Fangyu warns that genital warts cannot heal on their own and patients should not attempt to pick or squeeze them with bare hands. Without treatment with medication or surgery, the lesions can gradually grow larger and even spread to other parts of the body. Even if the warts are not visible to the naked eye, infected individuals can still unknowingly transmit the virus to others. You may also like The risk of infection is highest when the lesions are visible to the naked eye. Completing treatment does not guarantee that the disease will not recur. Dr. Gu Fangyu stated that in clinical practice, patients often experience multiple relapses after treatment. Therefore, in addition to treatment, monitoring and prevention are equally important. There are currently over 200 identified strains of HPV, divided into low-risk and high-risk strains based on their pathogenicity. Prolonged or repeated infections with high-risk HPV strains can progress to more serious conditions such as cervical cancer, anal cancer, penile cancer, vaginal cancer, and oropharyngeal cancer. HPV 疫苗接种 可以预防不良妊娠结局

HPV vaccination may protect against adverse pregnancy outcomes
新闻简报2026 年 10 月 7 日在《BMJ》上发表的一项瑞典大型研究发现,怀孕前感染 HPV 疫苗接种 与降低早产等主要妊娠并发症的风险相关。该研究基于 2006 年至 2023 年间瑞典 624,713 名 16-35 岁女性首次生育的​​单胎数据,结果显示,15% 的参与者在怀孕前接种了四价 HPV 疫苗接种。研究结果表明,HPV 疫苗接种 可能通过降低不良妊娠结局的风险,提供除癌症预防之外的其他益处。该研究强调,17 岁之前接种疫苗的女性风险降低更为显着。然而,研究人员强调,这项观察性研究无法建立明确的因果关系,他们承认自然流产数据不完整以及可能影响结果的未测量因素等局限性。尽管存在这些不确定性,该研究的高质量数据和长期随访有助于最大限度地减少偏差,支持妊娠期间 HPV 疫苗接种 的安全性和潜在益处。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026-10-07发表,分析的数据时间跨度为2006年至2023年。
地点与对象
该研究在瑞典进行,重点关注 16-35 岁女性的单胎、首次生育。
事件
《英国医学杂志》上发表的一项瑞典大型研究发现,怀孕前感染 HPV 疫苗接种 与降低早产等主要妊娠并发症的风险相关。研究人员认为,HPV 疫苗接种 的益处可能不仅限于预防癌症,还可以减少不良妊娠结局。
规模、影响与风险
该研究纳入了 624,713 名首次出生的单胎婴儿,其中 92,620 名(15%)在怀孕前接种了四价 HPV 疫苗接种。研究结果显示,早产和极早产、自发性早产、胎膜早破和产下婴儿过小有统计学上的显着关联。17 岁之前接种疫苗的女性风险降低更为明显。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是观察性的,因此无法得出关于因果关系的确切结论。作者承认有关自然流产的信息不完整,并且对潜在临床路径的研究有限。然而,他们表示该研究最大限度地减少了偏差和错误分类,从而能够评估常见和罕见的不良妊娠结局。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

HPV vaccination may protect against adverse pregnancy outcomes

News Brief
A large Swedish study published in The BMJ on October 7, 2026, found that HPV vaccination before pregnancy is associated with a lower risk of major pregnancy complications, including preterm birth. The research, based on data from 624,713 singleton, first-time births among women aged 16-35 years in Sweden between 2006 and 2023, revealed that 15% of the participants had received quadrivalent HPV vaccination prior to pregnancy. The findings suggest that HPV vaccination may offer additional benefits beyond cancer prevention by reducing the risk of adverse pregnancy outcomes. The study highlights that the risk reduction was more significant among women vaccinated before age 17. However, the researchers emphasize that this observational study cannot establish a definitive cause-and-effect relationship, and they acknowledge limitations such as incomplete data on spontaneous abortions and unmeasured factors that could influence the results. Despite these uncertainties, the study's high-quality data and long follow-up period help minimize bias, supporting the safety and potential benefits of HPV vaccination during pregnancy.
Original source text (English)
Having received the human papillomavirus (HPV) vaccine before pregnancy is associated with a lower risk of major pregnancy complications including preterm birth, with the greater reduction in risk seen among women vaccinated at an earlier age, finds a large Swedish study published by The BMJ today. The findings suggest that the benefits of HPV vaccination may extend beyond cancer prevention to the reduction of adverse pregnancy outcomes, say the researchers. HPV is one of the most common sexually transmitted infections. The quadrivalent HPV vaccine targets four types of HPV, including two (HPV types 16 and 18) that are responsible for about 70% of all cervical cancers. Growing evidence indicates that HPV infection, and the methods used to treat cervical lesions caused by HPV, are linked to an increased risk of pregnancy complications, including preterm birth, so preventing HPV holds potential benefits. To explore this further, researchers in Sweden used data from nationwide Swedish registers to evaluate the association between quadrivalent HPV vaccination before pregnancy and subsequent risk of adverse pregnancy outcomes. Their findings are based on 624,713 singleton, first-time births among women aged 16-35 years in Sweden between 2006 and 2023, of whom 92,620 (15%) had received quadrivalent human papillomavirus vaccination before pregnancy. Cases were women with at least one adverse pregnancy outcome, including preterm birth (before 37 weeks), very preterm birth (28 to 31 weeks), preterm and/or prelabor rupture of membranes, delivering a small or severely small baby, stillbirth, or newborn death. Each case was matched with up to 10 control women of the same age and year of conception and a range of other potentially influential factors were taken into account including self-reported cigarette smoking and body mass index at first antenatal visit, maternal diagnoses and assisted reproduction, mother's country of birth, family income and education level. After accounting for these factors, quadrivalent HPV vaccination was associated with lower odds of all included adverse pregnancy outcomes, compared with no vaccination. Statistically significant associations were seen for preterm and very preterm birth, spontaneous preterm birth, preterm prelabour rupture of membranes, and delivering a severely small baby. Risk reduction was generally more pronounced among women vaccinated before age 17. This is an observational study so no firm conclusions can be drawn about cause and effect, and the authors acknowledge incomplete information on spontaneous abortions, and limited investigation of the underlying clinical pathways. Nor can they rule out the possibility that other unmeasured factors may have affected their results. However, they say this large study based on high quality data with long follow-up in a real world setting minimized bias and misclassification, enabling assessment of both common and rare adverse pregnancy outcomes. And results were similar after further analyses, suggesting that they are robust. As such, they say: "Together with previous research showing the safety of human papillomavirus vaccination during pregnancy, our results suggest that human papillomavirus vaccination is not only safe with respect to pregnancy outcomes but may also confer additional benefits by reducing the risk of adverse events." "Collectively, this evidence supports the global strategy for human papillomavirus vaccination and its continued implementation in routine immunization programs," they add. Source: Journal reference

The researchers suggest that the benefits of HPV vaccination may extend beyond cancer prevention to reducing adverse pregnancy outcomes.
Scale, impact and risk

The findings showed statistically significant associations for preterm and very preterm birth, spontaneous preterm birth, preterm prelabour rupture of membranes, and delivering a severely small baby. The risk reduction was more pronounced among women vaccinated before age 17.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study is observational, so no firm conclusions about cause and effect can be drawn. The authors acknowledge incomplete information on spontaneous abortions and limited investigation of the underlying clinical pathways. However, they state that the study minimized bias and misclassification, enabling assessment of both common and rare adverse pregnancy outcomes.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

唾液腺肿瘤中 DAPK 基因甲基化状态及其与 HPV16/18 及 p16 表达的关系研究 - 生物通

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报A Scientific Reports study examined 45 salivary‐gland tumour specimens to assess DAPK gene methylation and its relationship with HPV16/18 and p16 expression. The cohort comprised roughly two‐thirds benign and one‐third malignant tumours. HPV16 L1 was detected in 68 % of samples, HPV16/18 E6 in 88 %, and p16 protein in 77 %, with higher expression rates observed in benign lesions. While DAPK methylation correlated with tumour type, no direct association with HPV markers was confirmed. The authors caution that the findings are exploratory, limited by sample size and reliance on immunohistochemistry without molecular HPV typing, and call for larger, PCR‐based investigations to clarify these relationships. No public‐health actions or outbreak implications were reported.
查看新闻五要素
时间
这篇文章发表于2026-10-08。所提供的证据中未报告基础研究或样本收集的日期。
地点与对象
The study examined 45 salivary‐gland tumour (SGT) samples (66.67 % benign, 33.34 % malignant). No specific geographic location of the sample collection or patient population is reported.
事件
《科学报告》中的一篇研究文章调查了 SGT 组织中 DAPK 基因的甲基化状态及其与 HPV16/18 和 p16 蛋白表达的关联。该杂志报道了该调查并由新闻网站总结;研究结果是探索性的,尚未得到证实。
规模、影响与风险
Among the 45 SGT samples, HPV16 L1 positivity was 68 %, HPV16/18 E6 positivity 88 %, and p16 protein expression 77 %. No cases of disease transmission, deaths, or broader public‐health impact were reported. The study suggests a possible link between DAPK methylation and tumour type, but the biological significance remains uncertain.
应对、状态与不确定性
作者将结果描述为初步结果,并指出了方法学的局限性,建议进一步开展基于 PCR 的 HPV 分型和功能验证的更大规模研究。没有表明采取公共卫生干预措施或官方应对行动。

View five news elements
Time
The article was published on 2026-10-08. The date of the underlying research or sample collection is not reported in the supplied evidence.
Location and population
The study examined 45 salivary‐gland tumour (SGT) samples (66.67 % benign, 33.34 % malignant). No specific geographic location of the sample collection or patient population is reported.
Event
A research article in Scientific Reports investigated the methylation status of the DAPK gene in SGT tissues and its association with HPV16/18 and p16 protein expression. The investigation was reported by the journal and summarized by the news site; the findings are presented as exploratory and not yet confirmed.
Scale, impact and risk
Among the 45 SGT samples, HPV16 L1 positivity was 68 %, HPV16/18 E6 positivity 88 %, and p16 protein expression 77 %. No cases of disease transmission, deaths, or broader public‐health impact were reported. The study suggests a possible link between DAPK methylation and tumour type, but the biological significance remains uncertain.
Response, status and uncertainty
The authors describe the results as preliminary and note methodological limitations, recommending further larger‐scale studies with PCR‐based HPV typing and functional validation. No public‐health interventions or official response actions are indicated.

研究显示 HPV 疫苗 与降低妊娠并发症风险有关

HPV vaccine linked to lower risk of pregnancy complications: Study
新闻简报英国医学杂志(BMJ)发表的一项研究发现,怀孕前接种 HPV 疫苗接种 可能会降低某些妊娠并发症的风险,例如早产前胎膜破裂、先兆子痫和产前出血。这项研究由瑞典科学家进行,分析了瑞典全国登记册的数据,涵盖 2006 年至 2023 年间 624,713 名 16-35 岁女性首次生育的​​独生子女。研究结果表明,较早接种 HPV 疫苗的女性风险降低幅度最大。该研究强调了 HPV 疫苗接种 除了预防癌症之外的潜在益处,表明它可能会改善妊娠结局。然而,结果基于观察数据,可能需要进一步的临床验证以确认其对公共卫生的影响。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2026年发表,研究结果基于2006年至2023年间收集的数据。发表日期为2026-10-08。
地点与对象
该研究分析了瑞典全国登记册的数据,纳入了瑞典 16-35 岁女性中 624,713 名首次生育的​​独生子女。
事件
英国医学杂志(BMJ)发表的一项研究发现,怀孕前接种 HPV 疫苗接种 可能会降低某些妊娠并发症的风险,例如早产前胎膜破裂、先兆子痫和产前出血。该研究由印度汉斯报道。
规模、影响与风险
研究发现,怀孕前接种HPV疫苗的女性可显着降低不良妊娠结局的风险,包括早产、早产前胎膜破裂、先兆子痫和产前出血。在较早年龄接种疫苗的人中,风险降低最为明显。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究是一项已发表的研究成果,其结果基于瑞典登记册的数据分析。研究结果表明,HPV 疫苗接种 的潜在益处超出了癌症预防的范围,但可能需要进一步研究或临床验证。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

HPV vaccine linked to lower risk of pregnancy complications: Study

News Brief
A study published in the British Medical Journal (BMJ) found that HPV vaccination before pregnancy may reduce the risk of certain pregnancy complications, such as preterm prelabour rupture of membranes, pre-eclampsia, and antepartum haemorrhage. The research, conducted by Swedish scientists, analyzed data from nationwide Swedish registers covering 624,713 single-child, first-time births among women aged 16-35 years between 2006 and 2023. The findings indicate that women immunized against HPV at an earlier age experienced the greatest risk reduction. The study highlights the potential benefits of HPV vaccination beyond cancer prevention, suggesting it may improve pregnancy outcomes. However, the results are based on observational data and may require further clinical validation to confirm their implications for public health.
Original source text (English)

HPV vaccine linked to lower risk of pregnancy complications: Study In Short Human papillomavirus (HPV) is a common sexually transmitted infection. Persistent, high-risk infections can cause cervical cancer, among other types The Hans India Created On: 9 Oct 2026 1:17 AM ISTComment Share : X HPV vaccine linked to lower risk of pregnancy complications: Study A− Font size A+ Select Hans India Now FOLLOW ON Google News New Delhi: Getting vaccinated against HPV before pregnancy could be related with a lower risk of major pregnancy complications including preterm birth, suggesting that benefits of the HPV vaccine may extend beyond cancer prevention, according to Swedish research. Findings published in the British Medical Journal (BMJ) show that the greatest benefits in risk reduction were seen among women immunised at an earlier age. Human papillomavirus (HPV) is a common sexually transmitted infection. Persistent, high-risk infections can cause cervical cancer, among other types. Researchers, including those at Sweden’s Karolinska Institutet, said a growing evidence indicates that HPV infection, and the methods used to treat cervical lesions caused by HPV, are linked to an increased risk of pregnancy complications, including preterm birth, and therefore, preventing HPV holds potential benefits. A 2025 study from Aberdeen in the UK, published in the European Journal of Obstetrics and Gynecology and Reproductive Biology, analysed 9,200 women with a pregnancy recorded between 2006 and 2020. It found that while there was no difference in rates of spontaneous preterm birth according to HPV vaccination status, other outcomes, including preterm prelabour rupture of membranes, pre-eclampsia and antepartum haemorrhage, may be significantly reduced in women immunised against HPV. The team used data from nationwide Swedish registers to look at 624,713 single-child, first-time births among women aged 16-35 years between 2006 and 2023, of whom 92,620 (15 per cent) had received a quadrivalent human papillomavirus vaccination before pregnancy. The quadrivalent HPV vaccine targets four types of HPV, including two (HPV types 16 and 18) that are responsible for about 70 per cent of all cervical cancers. Adverse pregnancy outcomes that were looked at included preterm birth (before 37 weeks), very preterm birth (28 to 31 weeks), preterm and prelabour rupture of membranes, delivering a small or severely small baby, stillbirth, or newborn death. View five news elements
Time
The study was published in 2026, with the findings based on data collected between 2006 and 2023. The publication date is 2026-10-08.
Location and population
The study analyzed data from nationwide Swedish registers and included 624,713 single-child, first-time births among women aged 16-35 years in Sweden.
Event
A study published in the British Medical Journal (BMJ) found that HPV vaccination before pregnancy may reduce the risk of certain pregnancy complications, such as preterm prelabour rupture of membranes, pre-eclampsia, and antepartum haemorrhage. The study was reported by The Hans India.
Scale, impact and risk
The study found that women immunized against HPV before pregnancy had a significantly reduced risk of adverse pregnancy outcomes, including preterm birth, preterm prelabour rupture of membranes, pre-eclampsia, and antepartum haemorrhage. The risk reduction was most pronounced among those vaccinated at an earlier age.
Response, status and uncertainty
The study is a published research finding, and the results are based on data analysis from Swedish registers. The findings suggest potential benefits of HPV vaccination beyond cancer prevention, but further investigation or clinical validation may be needed.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

HPV 疫苗接种 和 宫颈癌 预防:公共卫生的最大机会之一 - ACCESS Newswire

HPV Vaccination and Cervical Cancer Prevention: One of Public Health's Greatest Opportunities - ACCESS Newswire
新闻简报ACCESS Newswire 于 2026 年 10 月 6 日发表的文章讨论了 HPV 疫苗接种 在预防 宫颈癌 和其他 HPV 相关疾病方面的重要作用。它强调,几乎所有 宫颈癌 都是由某些 HPV 类型的持续感染引起的,而 疫苗(称为 Gardasil)可以预防这些侵袭性菌株。该文章强调,较高的 疫苗接种 覆盖范围与降低的 宫颈癌 风险相关。值得注意的是,《柳叶刀》发表的一项研究发现,在 HPV 疫苗接种 率较高的英格兰,2020 年至 2024 年间,20 至 24 岁女性中没有发生 宫颈癌 死亡,而根据历史趋势预计死亡人数将超过 23 例。文章还指出,美国每年仍有数千人被诊断出患有 HPV 相关癌症,这凸显了对 疫苗接种 和筛查计划的持续需求。这一信息反映了 HPV 疫苗接种 作为主要公共卫生机会的潜力,特别是在降低可预防的癌症风险方面。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该文章发表于2026年10月6日,讨论了2020年至2024年的数据。
地点与对象
美国和英国,重点关注英国20岁至24岁的女性。
事件
该文章强调了 HPV 疫苗接种 在预防 宫颈癌 和其他 HPV 相关癌症中的作用,并引用了《柳叶刀》上发表的一项研究,该研究发现 2020 年至 2024 年间,英格兰 20 至 24 岁女性中没有 宫颈癌 死亡病例。
规模、影响与风险
文章指出,更高的 疫苗接种 覆盖范围与更大程度地降低 宫颈癌 风险相关。它还提到,美国每年仍有数千名女性和男性被诊断出患有 HPV 相关癌症。
应对、状态与不确定性
该文章报告了 HPV 疫苗接种 的影响及其有效性,但没有提及正在进行的调查或不确定性。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

HPV Vaccination and Cervical Cancer Prevention: One of Public Health's Greatest Opportunities - ACCESS Newswire

News Brief
The article published by ACCESS Newswire on October 6, 2026, discusses the significant role of HPV vaccination in preventing cervical cancer and other HPV-related diseases. It highlights that nearly all cervical cancers are caused by persistent infection with certain HPV types, and the vaccine, known as Gardasil, protects against these aggressive strains. The piece emphasizes that higher vaccination coverage correlates with reduced cervical cancer risk. Notably, a study published in The Lancet found that in England, where HPV vaccination rates are high, no cervical cancer deaths occurred among women aged 20 to 24 between 2020 and 2024, compared to over 23 expected deaths based on historical trends. The article also notes that thousands of individuals in the United States continue to be diagnosed with HPV-related cancers annually, underscoring the ongoing need for vaccination and screening programs. This information reflects the potential of HPV vaccination as a major public health opportunity, particularly in reducing preventable cancer risks.
